int PHYSFS_ucs4stricmp (const PHYSFS_uint32 * str1, const PHYSFS_uint32 * str2)

Comparación insensible a mayúsculas/minúsculas de dos cadenas UCS-4. Este es un reemplazo de strcasecmp/stricmp que espera que ambas cadenas estén en formato UCS-4 (también conocido como UTF-32). Realizará un "ajuste de mayúsculas/minúsculas" para determinar si los códigos Unicode en las cadenas coinciden.

Devolverá qué cadena es "mayor" que la otra, pero ten en cuenta que esto no tiene por qué significar nada: 'a' puede ser "menor" que 'b', pero un kanji japonés no tiene una relación alfabética significativa con una lambda griega, pero la capacidad de asignar un valor confiable permite algoritmos de ordenación, aunque no sean completamente racionales. En la mayoría de los casos, debes tratar el valor devuelto como "igual" o "no igual".

Al igual que stricmp, espera que ambas cadenas terminen en NULL.

Parámetros

str1 La primera cadena a comparar.
str2 La segunda cadena a comparar.

Devuelve

-1 si str1 es "menor" que str2, 1 si es "mayor", 0 si son iguales.

int PHYSFS_unmount (const char * oldDir)

Elimina un directorio o archivo comprimido de la ruta de búsqueda. Esta función es funcionalmente equivalente a PHYSFS_removeFromSearchPath(), pero se ha mantenido esta función para mantener la terminología asociada con PHYSFS_mount().

Debes proporcionar una coincidencia (no solo una coincidencia de mayúsculas/minúsculas) con un directorio o archivo comprimido que ya esté en la ruta de búsqueda, especificado en notación dependiente de la plataforma.

Esta llamada fallará (y no eliminará de la ruta) si el elemento todavía tiene archivos abiertos.

Advertencia

Esta función espera la ruta del directorio o archivo comprimido que fue montado (la misma cadena usada en el argumento de "newDir" de PHYSFS_addToSearchPath o en cualquiera de las funciones de montaje), no la ruta donde fue montado en la jerarquía (el "mountPoint" de cualquiera de las funciones de montaje).

Parámetros

oldDir El directorio o archivo comprimido a eliminar.

Devuelve

Un valor distinto de cero en caso de éxito, cero en caso de error. Usa PHYSFS_getLastErrorCode() para obtener el error específico.

Ver también

PHYSFS_getSearchPath

PHYSFS_mount

int PHYSFS_utf16stricmp (const PHYSFS_uint16 * str1, const PHYSFS_uint16 * str2)

Comparación insensible a mayúsculas/minúsculas de dos cadenas UTF-16. Este es un reemplazo de strcasecmp/stricmp que espera que ambas cadenas estén en formato UTF-16. Realizará un "ajuste de mayúsculas/minúsculas" para determinar si los códigos Unicode en las cadenas coinciden.

Devolverá qué cadena es "mayor" que la otra, pero ten en cuenta que esto no tiene por qué significar nada: 'a' puede ser "menor" que 'b', pero un kanji japonés no tiene una relación alfabética significativa con una lambda griega, pero la capacidad de asignar un valor confiable permite algoritmos de ordenación, aunque no sean completamente racionales. En la mayoría de los casos, debes tratar el valor devuelto como "igual" o "no igual".

Al igual que stricmp, espera que ambas cadenas terminen en NULL.

Parámetros

str1 La primera cadena a comparar.
str2 La segunda cadena a comparar.

Devuelve

-1 si str1 es "menor" que str2, 1 si es "mayor", 0 si son iguales.

int PHYSFS_strcasecmp (const char * str1, const char * str2)

Case-insensitive compare of two ASCII strings. This is a strcasecmp/stricmp replacement. It expects both strings to be composed of only low-ASCII characters (0-127).

It will report which string is "greater than" the other, but be aware that this doesn't necessarily mean anything: 'a' may be "less than" 'b', but a Japanese kuten has no meaningful alphabetically relationship to a Greek lambda, but being able to assign a reliable "value" makes sorting algorithms possible, if not entirely sane. Most cases should treat the return value as "equal" or "not equal".

Like stricmp, this expects both strings to be NULL-terminated.

Parameters

str1 First string to compare.
str2 Second string to compare.

Returns

-1 if str1 is "less than" str2, 1 if "greater than", 0 if equal.

void PHYSFS_utf8FromLatin1 (const char * src, char * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a Latin1 string to a UTF-8 string. Latin1 strings are 8-bits per character: a popular "high ASCII" encoding.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 expands latin1 codepoints over 127 from 1 to 2 bytes, so the string may grow in some cases.

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UTF-8 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Please note that we do not supply a UTF-8 to Latin1 converter, since Latin1 can't express most Unicode codepoints. It's a legacy encoding; you should be converting away from it at all times.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in Latin1 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UTF-8 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

void PHYSFS_utf8FromUcs2 (const PHYSFS_uint16 * src, char * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UCS-2 string to a UTF-8 string.

Warning

you almost certainly should use PHYSFS_utf8FromUtf16(), which became available in PhysicsFS 2.1, unless you know what you're doing.

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UCS-2 values in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UCS-2 strings are 16-bits per character: TCHAR on Windows, when building with Unicode support. Please note that modern versions of Windows use UTF-16, which is an extended form of UCS-2, and not UCS-2 itself. You almost certainly want PHYSFS_utf8FromUtf16() instead.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 never uses more than 32-bits per character, so while it may shrink a UCS-2 string, it may also expand it.

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UTF-8 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UCS-2 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UTF-8 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

See also

PHYSFS_utf8FromUtf16

void PHYSFS_utf8FromUcs4 (const PHYSFS_uint32 * src, char * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UCS-4 string to a UTF-8 string.

Warning

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UCS-4 values in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UCS-4 (aka UTF-32) strings are 32-bits per character: wchar_t on Unix.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is the same size as the source buffer. UTF-8 never uses more than 32-bits per character, so while it may shrink a UCS-4 string, it will never expand it.

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UTF-8 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UCS-4 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UTF-8 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

void PHYSFS_utf8FromUtf16 (const PHYSFS_uint16 * src, char * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UTF-16 string to a UTF-8 string.

Warning

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UTF-16 sequences in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UTF-16 strings are 16-bits per character (except some chars, which are 32-bits): TCHAR on Windows, when building with Unicode support. Modern Windows releases use UTF-16. Windows releases before 2000 used TCHAR, but only handled UCS-2. UTF-16 is UCS-2, except for the characters that are 4 bytes, which aren't representable in UCS-2 at all anyhow. If you aren't sure, you should be using UTF-16 at this point on Windows.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 never uses more than 32-bits per character, so while it may shrink a UTF-16 string, it may also expand it.

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UTF-8 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UTF-16 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UTF-8 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

int PHYSFS_utf8stricmp (const char * str1, const char * str2)

Case-insensitive compare of two UTF-8 strings. This is a strcasecmp/stricmp replacement that expects both strings to be in UTF-8 encoding. It will do "case folding" to decide if the Unicode codepoints in the strings match.

If both strings are exclusively low-ASCII characters, this will do the right thing, as that is also valid UTF-8. If there are any high-ASCII chars, this will not do what you expect!

It will report which string is "greater than" the other, but be aware that this doesn't necessarily mean anything: 'a' may be "less than" 'b', but a Japanese kuten has no meaningful alphabetically relationship to a Greek lambda, but being able to assign a reliable "value" makes sorting algorithms possible, if not entirely sane. Most cases should treat the return value as "equal" or "not equal".

Like stricmp, this expects both strings to be NULL-terminated.

Parameters

str1 First string to compare.
str2 Second string to compare.

Returns

-1 if str1 is "less than" str2, 1 if "greater than", 0 if equal.

void PHYSFS_utf8ToUcs2 (const char * src, PHYSFS_uint16 * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UTF-8 string to a UCS-2 string.

Warning

you almost certainly should use PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16(), which became available in PhysicsFS 2.1, unless you know what you're doing.

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UTF-8 sequences in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UCS-2 strings are 16-bits per character: TCHAR on Windows, when building with Unicode support. Please note that modern versions of Windows use UTF-16, which is an extended form of UCS-2, and not UCS-2 itself. You almost certainly want PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16() instead, but you need to understand how that changes things, too.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 uses from one to four bytes per character, but UCS-2 always uses two, so an entirely low-ASCII string will double in size!

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UCS-2 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UTF-8 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UCS-2 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

See also

PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16

void PHYSFS_utf8ToUcs4 (const char * src, PHYSFS_uint32 * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UTF-8 string to a UCS-4 string.

Warning

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UTF-8 sequences in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UCS-4 (aka UTF-32) strings are 32-bits per character: wchar_t on Unix.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is four times the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 uses from one to four bytes per character, but UCS-4 always uses four, so an entirely low-ASCII string will quadruple in size!

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UCS-4 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UTF-8 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UCS-4 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

void PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16 (const char * src, PHYSFS_uint16 * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UTF-8 string to a UTF-16 string.

Warning

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UTF-8 sequences in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UTF-16 strings are 16-bits per character (except some chars, which are 32-bits): TCHAR on Windows, when building with Unicode support. Modern Windows releases use UTF-16. Windows releases before 2000 used TCHAR, but only handled UCS-2. UTF-16 is UCS-2, except for the characters that are 4 bytes, which aren't representable in UCS-2 at all anyhow. If you aren't sure, you should be using UTF-16 at this point on Windows.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 uses from one to four bytes per character, but UTF-16 always uses two (mostly), so an entirely low-ASCII string will double in size!

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UTF-16 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Null-terminated source string in UTF-8 format.
dst Buffer to store converted UTF-16 string.
len Size, in bytes, of destination buffer.

void PHYSFS_utf8ToUcs4 (const char * src, PHYSFS_uint32 * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convert a UTF-8 string to a UCS-4 string.

Warning

This function will not report an error if there are invalid UTF-8 sequences in the source string. It will replace them with a '?' character and continue on.

UCS-4 strings are 32-bits per character.

To ensure that the destination buffer is large enough for the conversion, please allocate a buffer that is double the size of the source buffer. UTF-8 uses from one to four bytes per character, but UCS-4 always uses four bytes. For example, a string with only ASCII characters (which normally take 1 byte in UTF-8) would take 4 bytes in UCS-4, doubling its size. If you aren't sure, you should allocate 4x the space of the source buffer to be safe.

Strings that don't fit in the destination buffer will be truncated, but will always be null-terminated and never have an incomplete UCS-4 sequence at the end. If the buffer length is 0, this function does nothing.

Parameters

src Cadena de origen terminada en nulo en formato UTF-8.
dst Búfer para almacenar la cadena UCS-4 convertida.
len Tamaño, en bytes, del búfer de destino.

void PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16 (const char * src, PHYSFS_uint16 * dst, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Convierte una cadena UTF-8 a una cadena UTF-16.

Advertencia

Esta función no informará de un error si hay secuencias UTF-8 inválidas en la cadena de origen. Las reemplazará con un carácter '?' y continuará.

Las cadenas UTF-16 tienen 16 bits por carácter (excepto algunos caracteres, que tienen 32 bits): TCHAR en Windows, cuando se compila con soporte Unicode. Las versiones modernas de Windows usan UTF-16. Las versiones de Windows anteriores al 2000 usaban TCHAR, pero solo soportaban UCS-2. UTF-16 *es* UCS-2, excepto por los caracteres que tienen 4 bytes, que de todos modos no son representables en UCS-2. Si no estás seguro, deberías estar usando UTF-16 en este momento en Windows.

Para asegurar que el búfer de destino sea lo suficientemente grande para la conversión, por favor asigna un búfer que sea el doble del tamaño del búfer de origen. UTF-8 usa de uno a cuatro bytes por carácter, pero UTF-16 siempre usa de dos a cuatro, ¡así que una cadena completamente ASCII de bajo nivel duplicará su tamaño! Los caracteres UTF-16 que ocuparían cuatro bytes también ocupan cuatro bytes en UTF-8, por lo que no necesitas asignar 4x el espacio por si acaso: el doble será suficiente.

Las cadenas que no quepan en el búfer de destino serán truncadas, pero siempre estarán terminadas en nulo y nunca tendrán un par substituto UTF-16 incompleto al final. Si la longitud del búfer es 0, esta función no hace nada.

Parámetros

src Cadena de origen terminada en nulo en formato UTF-8.
dst Búfer para almacenar la cadena UTF-16 convertida.
len Tamaño, en bytes, del búfer de destino.

Ver también

PHYSFS_utf8ToUtf16

PHYSFS_sint64 PHYSFS_write (PHYSFS_File * handle, const void * buffer, PHYSFS_uint32 objSize, PHYSFS_uint32 objCount)

Escribe datos en un manejador de archivo PhysicsFS. El archivo debe estar abierto para escritura.

Obsoleto

Desde PhysicsFS 2.1, usa PHYSFS_writeBytes() en su lugar. Esta función simplemente lo envuelve de todos modos. Esta función nunca aclaró qué sucedería si lograbas escribir un objeto parcial, por lo que trabajar a nivel de byte lo hace más limpio para todos, especialmente ahora que las interfaces PHYSFS_Io pueden ser suministradas por la aplicación.

Parámetros

handle valor de retorno de PHYSFS_openWrite() o PHYSFS_openAppend().
buffer búfer de bytes para escribir en (handle).
objSize tamaño en bytes de los objetos que se están escribiendo en (handle).
objCount número de objetos (de tamaño objSize) que se están escribiendo en (handle).

Retorna

número de objetos escritos. PHYSFS_getLastErrorCode() puede arrojar luz sobre la razón por la que esto podría ser < (objCount). -1 en caso de fallo completo.

Ver también

PHYSFS_writeBytes

PHYSFS_sint64 PHYSFS_writeBytes (PHYSFS_File * handle, const void * buffer, PHYSFS_uint64 len)

Escribe datos en un manejador de archivo PhysicsFS. El archivo debe estar abierto para escritura.

Ten en cuenta que aunque (len) es un entero sin signo de 64 bits, estás limitado a 63 bits (9223372036854775807 bytes), por lo que podemos devolver un valor negativo en caso de error. Si la longitud es mayor que 0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF, esta función fallará inmediatamente. Para sistemas sin un tipo de dato de 64 bits, estás limitado a 31 bits (0x7FFFFFFF, o 2147483647 bytes). Confiamos en que la mayoría de las cosas no necesitarán hacer múltiples gigabytes de i/o en una sola llamada de todos modos, ¡pero ¿por qué limitar las cosas?

Parámetros

handle valor de retorno de PHYSFS_openWrite() o PHYSFS_openAppend().
buffer búfer de (len) bytes para escribir en (handle).
len número de bytes que se están escribiendo en (handle).

Retorna

número de bytes escritos. Esto puede ser menor que (len); en caso de error, el sistema puede intentar escribir tantos bytes como sea posible, por lo que puede ocurrir una escritura incompleta. PHYSFS_getLastErrorCode() puede arrojar luz sobre la razón por la que esto podría ser < (len). -1 en caso de fallo completo.

int PHYSFS_writeSBE16 (PHYSFS_File * file, PHYSFS_sint16 val)

Convierte y escribe un valor de 16 bits con signo en bigendian. Función de conveniencia. Convierte un valor de 16 bits con signo del orden de bytes nativo de la plataforma a bigendian y lo escribe en un archivo.

Parámetros

file Manejador de archivo PhysicsFS al que escribir.
val Valor a convertir y escribir.

Retorna

cero en caso de fallo, distinto de cero en caso de éxito. En caso de fallo, puedes averiguar qué salió mal desde PHYSFS_getLastErrorCode().
